Let me compile a comprehensive report based on what I've gathered.\\n\\n## OpenVLA-OFT Model Architecture: Token Importance Analysis Summary\\n\\nBased on my exploration of the OpenVLA-OFT repository, here's the comprehensive information for your token importance analysis:\\n\\n### **1. Model Loading**\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_vla(cfg: Any) -> torch.nn.Module:\\n    # For local checkpoints, register with Hugging Face\\n    if not model_is_on_hf_hub(cfg.pretrained_checkpoint):\\n        AutoConfig.register(\\\"openvla\\\", OpenVLAConfig)\\n        AutoImageProcessor.register(OpenVLAConfig, PrismaticImageProcessor)\\n        AutoProcessor.register(OpenVLAConfig, PrismaticProcessor)\\n        AutoModelForVision2Seq.register(OpenVLAConfig, OpenVLAForActionPrediction)\\n        update_auto_map(cfg.pretrained_checkpoint)\\n        check_model_logic_mismatch(cfg.pretrained_checkpoint)\\n    \\n    # Load model with specified precision and quantization\\n    vla = AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(\\n        cfg.pretrained_checkpoint,\\n        torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16,\\n        load_in_8bit=cfg.load_in_8bit,\\n        load_in_4bit=cfg.load_in_4bit,\\n        low_cpu_mem_usage=True,\\n        trust_remote_code=True,\\n    )\\n    \\n    # Optional FiLM wrapping for vision backbone\\n    if cfg.use_film:\\n        vla = _apply_film_to_vla(vla, cfg)\\n    \\n    vla.vision_backbone.set_num_images_in_input(cfg.num_images_in_input)\\n    vla.eval()\\n    \\n    # Move to device unless quantized\\n    if not cfg.load_in_8bit and not cfg.load_in_4bit:\\n        vla = vla.to(DEVICE)\\n    \\n    _load_dataset_stats(vla, cfg.pretrained_checkpoint)\\n    return vla\\n```\\n\\n**Key Detail**: The model uses `AutoModelForVision2Seq` which is mapped to `OpenVLAForActionPrediction` class.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **2. Action Head Architecture**\\n\\nThe code supports two distinct action head types:\\n\\n```python\\ndef get_action_head(cfg: Any, llm_dim: int) -> Union[L1RegressionActionHead, DiffusionActionHead]:\\n    \\n    # L1 Regression head (continuous action prediction)\\n    if cfg.use_l1_regression:\\n        action_head = L1RegressionActionHead(\\n            input_dim=llm_dim,          # LLM hidden dimension\\n            hidden_dim=llm_dim,         # MLP hidden dimension\\n            action_dim=ACTION_DIM       # 7D for robot (6D pose + 1D gripper)\\n        )\\n    \\n    # Diffusion-based head (noise prediction)\\n    elif cfg.use_diffusion:\\n        action_head = DiffusionActionHead(\\n            input_dim=llm_dim,\\n            hidden_dim=llm_dim,\\n            action_dim=ACTION_DIM,\\n            num_diffusion_steps_train=cfg.num_diffusion_steps_train\\n        )\\n        action_head.noise_scheduler.set_timesteps(cfg.num_diffusion_steps_inference)\\n    \\n    action_head = action_head.to(torch.bfloat16).to(DEVICE)\\n    action_head.eval()\\n    \\n    # Load checkpoint weights\\n    if model_is_on_hf_hub(cfg.pretrained_checkpoint):\\n        action_head_path = hf_hub_download(...)\\n        state_dict = load_component_state_dict(action_head_path)\\n    else:\\n        checkpoint_path = find_checkpoint_file(cfg.pretrained_checkpoint, \\\"action_head\\\")\\n        state_dict = load_component_state_dict(checkpoint_path)\\n    \\n    action_head.load_state_dict(state_dict)\\n    return action_head\\n```\\n\\n**Key Details**:\\n- **L1Regression**: Simple MLP taking LLM hidden states → continuous actions, optimized with L1 loss\\n- **Diffusion**: Iterative noise prediction following DDIM sampling schedule\\n- Both heads take LLM-dimension embeddings as input (hidden states)\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **3. Forward Pass and Action Loss**\\n\\n```python\\n# Training forward pass with full output structure\\noutput: CausalLMOutputWithPast = vla(\\n    input_ids=batch[\\\"input_ids\\\"].to(device_id),\\n    attention_mask=batch[\\\"attention_mask\\\"].to(device_id),\\n    pixel_values=batch[\\\"pixel_values\\\"].to(torch.bfloat16).to(device_id),\\n    labels=batch[\\\"labels\\\"],\\n    output_hidden_states=True,  # CRITICAL: Retrieve hidden states for action heads\\n    proprio=batch[\\\"proprio\\\"] if use_proprio else None,\\n    proprio_projector=proprio_projector if use_proprio else None,\\n    noisy_actions=noisy_actions if use_diffusion else None,\\n    noisy_action_projector=noisy_action_projector if use_diffusion else None,\\n    diffusion_timestep_embeddings=diffusion_timestep_embeddings if use_diffusion else None,\\n    use_film=use_film,\\n)\\n\\n# Extract hidden states and compute action loss\\nlast_hidden_states = output.hidden_states[-1]  # (B, seq_len, D)\\n\\n# Action loss computation (three modes):\\n# 1. Discrete tokenization: cross-entropy loss on action tokens\\n# 2. L1 regression: torch.nn.L1Loss()(pred_actions, gt_actions)\\n# 3. Diffusion: MSE loss on noise prediction\\n\\nloss, metrics = compute_action_loss(output, action_head, ...)\\nnormalized_loss = loss / grad_accumulation_steps\\nnormalized_loss.backward()\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **4. Vision Token Extraction**\\n\\n```python\\n# From training pipeline:\\nlast_hidden_states = output.hidden_states[-1]  # Full sequence: (B, seq_len, D)\\n\\n# Vision tokens occupy early positions\\nnum_patches = vla.vision_backbone.num_patches  # Computed based on all input images\\n\\n# Extract different portions:\\nvision_hidden_states = last_hidden_states[:, :num_patches, :]      # Vision only\\ntext_hidden_states = last_hidden_states[:, num_patches:-1, :]      # Text (excludes action response)\\naction_hidden_states = last_hidden_states[:, num_patches:-1, :]    # Further masked for action portion\\n\\n# Num patches accounts for:\\n# - Vision patches from all images\\n# - Proprioceptive embeddings (if enabled)\\n# - Diffusion timestep embeddings (if enabled)\\n```\\n\\n**Key Detail**: Vision information is packed at the beginning of the token sequence, allowing straightforward indexing via `num_patches`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **5. Using output_attentions=True**\\n\\nTo retrieve attention weights alongside hidden states:\\n\\n```python\\n# Modify forward call to include attention outputs:\\noutput = vla(\\n    input_ids=batch[\\\"input_ids\\\"].to(device_id),\\n    attention_mask=batch[\\\"attention_mask\\\"].to(device_id),\\n    pixel_values=batch[\\\"pixel_values\\\"].to(torch.bfloat16).to(device_id),\\n    labels=batch[\\\"labels\\\"],\\n    output_hidden_states=True,      # For hidden states\\n    output_attentions=True,         # ADD THIS for attention weights\\n    proprio=batch[\\\"proprio\\\"] if use_proprio else None,\\n    # ... other parameters ...\\n)\\n\\n# Access attention weights:\\nattentions = output.attentions  # Tuple of attention tensors per layer\\n# attentions[i] shape: (batch_size, num_heads, seq_len, seq_len)\\n\\n# Hidden states remain available:\\nhidden_states = output.hidden_states  # Tuple of hidden tensors per layer\\n# hidden_states[i] shape: (batch_size, seq_len, hidden_dim)\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **6. The key difference for OFT is:\\n1. LoRA adapter on the LLM\\n2. Separate L1 action head\\n3. Separate proprio projector\\n\\nFor the token importance analysis, I only need the vision backbone + projector + LLM forward pass. \\n\\nThe action head is separate, so I need to figure out whether the OFT checkpoint has the LoRA weights baked into the base model files or if they're stored separately in the adapter directory. Looking at the structure, it seems like the safetensor files contain the base weights while the LoRA adapter is in its own subdirectory.\\n\\nWhen loading with `AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained`, it probably won't automatically merge the LoRA weights since they're in a separate folder—I'd need to use PEFT to apply them. But actually, for the analysis I'm doing, the vision backbone and projector weights are what matter most, and those should be the same between the standard OpenVLA model and the OFT version since LoRA only tunes the LLM part.\\n\\nFor the token importance analysis, the vision backbone and projector are frozen in both models, while the LLM is fine-tuned differently (full fine-tuning vs LoRA) and the action heads differ. This means the vision tokens themselves are identical between the two models for the same input, but the LLM processes them differently. The pruning curves will reflect how much the LLM's output degrades when vision tokens are removed, which depends on each model's LLM weights. Similarly, attention patterns will differ based on the LLM weights.\\n\\nSince the OFT model requires the `prismatic` package, I'm weighing whether to install it or take a different approach.\\n\\nOption C seems most practical: use the standard OpenVLA model as the base for analysis, then manually merge the LoRA weights into the OFT model's LLM to avoid the dependency issue entirely. This hinges on whether the safetensor files differ between the two models or if the fine-tuning is applied separately. Looking at the file sizes and checkpoint structure, I think the OFT checkpoint might already have merged weights, but I'm not entirely sure. I'll take a hybrid approach: use the standard OpenVLA model that's already available, then download just the LoRA adapter and action head from HuggingFace to apply on top of it, giving me the OFT model without needing the full weights.\\n\\nActually, the adapter config is also an LFS pointer, so that won't work either. The simpler solution is to download the complete OFT model directly from HuggingFace using `snapshot_download`, then copy over the self-contained modeling code from the standard OpenVLA directory. Since both models use the same architecture (OpenVLAForActionPrediction), the OFT checkpoint should load fine with that code—the main difference is that OFT has LoRA baked in during training, and for my analysis purposes I don't need the OFT-specific constants.\\n\\nBut wait, I need to reconsider whether the weight structure actually differs. The OFT model predicts multi-step action chunks while standard OpenVLA does single-step actions, so the architectures might not be as compatible as I thought. Let me check the configs more carefully to see if they're actually the same model type or if there are structural differences I'm missing. They use the same class! The OFT model just has additional components (LoRA, action head, proprio projector) that are loaded separately.\\n\\nSo the approach is:\\n1. Download the OFT model safetensors from HuggingFace\\n2. Use the standard OpenVLA modeling code to load it\\n3. The base model weights should be compatible\\n\\nBut wait, if the OFT model has LoRA applied, the safetensors might contain the merged weights. Let me think...\\n\\nFor HuggingFace checkpoints of OpenVLA-OFT, the weights are:\\n- Base model (Llama-2 7B + vision backbone + projector) in the 4 safetensor shards\\n- These are the original pretrained weights (NOT fine-tuned)\\n- The LoRA adapter is in a separate subdirectory containing the fine-tuned delta\\n\\nSo to get the full OFT model, I need to load the base weights and merge in the LoRA.\\n\\nFor the token importance analysis, the vision backbone and projector are frozen, so the projected vision tokens are identical to standard OpenVLA. The LoRA only affects the LLM attention patterns. This means I can use the standard OpenVLA model for L2 norm computation since that depends only on the frozen projector, but I need the LoRA-merged OFT model for the pruning curve loss and attention entropy calculations since those depend on the LLM weights.\\n\\nThe loss computation uses cross-entropy on action token generation as a proxy for vision token importance. Given the practical constraints—the standard OpenVLA model is readily available while the OFT model requires downloading ~14GB—I should just download the OFT model directly to the cache directory, which should take 10-20 minutes on this server. The cleanest approach is to download the OFT model and then copy over the standard model components where needed. For the pruning curve comparison, what really matters is how each model responds when vision tokens get removed—since the vision backbone is frozen in both cases, the key difference will be how the LLM component (full fine-tuning vs LoRA) handles the degraded input.\\n\\nThe autoregressive approach generates actions sequentially with attention to vision tokens, while the L1 head relies on final hidden states, so they'll likely show different sensitivity patterns. Rather than trying to track down all the missing dependencies, I'll just install the openvla-oft package directly or define those constants inline to get the comparison The key difference is that standard OpenVLA uses cross-entropy loss on discrete action tokens while OFT uses L1 loss on continuous action outputs, so I need to find a common metric to fairly compare their pruning curves.\\n\\nI'll use normalized loss (loss at pruning level k divided by baseline) as the primary metric, supplemented by representation-level degradation through hidden state similarity. For now I'll focus on the standard OpenVLA model and can extend to OFT later. numpy as np\\\\\\\\nimport timm\\\\\\\\nimport tokenizers\\\\\\\\nimport torch\\\\\\\\nimport torch.nn as nn\\\\\\\\nimport transformers\\\\\\\\nfrom timm.models.vision_transformer import LayerScale\\\\\\\\nfrom transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, PretrainedConfig, PreTrainedModel\\\\\\\\nfrom transformers.modeling_outputs import ModelOutput\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nfrom .configuration_prismatic import OpenVLAConfig, PrismaticConfig\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n# Get Logger\\\\\\\\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n# === PyTorch/HuggingFace Default IGNORE_INDEX (for CrossEntropyLoss labels)\\\\\\\\nIGNORE_INDEX = -100\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n# === Utility Functions for Monkey-Patching ===\\\\\\\\ndef unpack_tuple(fn: Callable[[Any], Tuple[Any]]) -> Callable[[Any], Any]:\\\\\\\\n    def wrapper(*args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:\\\\\\\\n        result = fn(*args, **kwargs)\\\\\\\\n        return result[0] if isinstance(result, (tuple, list)) else result\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    return wrapper\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n# HF Transformers overwrites parameters with names containing `gamma`; we're going to patch VisionBackbone.LayerScale.\\\\\\\\n#   =>> TIMM :: https://github.com/huggingface/pytorch-image-models/blob/main/timm/models/vision_transformer.py#L109\\\\\\\\n#   =>> Transformers :: https://github.com/huggingface/transformers/blob/main/src/transformers/modeling_utils.py#L3960\\\\\\\\ndef _ls_new_forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\\\\\\\\n    return x.mul_(self.scale_factor) if self.inplace else x * self.scale_factor\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\ndef ls_apply_patch(ls_module: LayerScale):\\\\\\\\n    ls_module.scale_factor = nn.Parameter(ls_module.gamma.clone())\\\\\\\\n    ls_module.forward = _ls_new_forward.__get__(ls_module, LayerScale)\\\\\\\\n    del ls_module.gamma\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n# === Prismatic Vision Backbone (nn.Module) Definitions (w/ Fused Backbone Support) ===\\\\\\\\nclass PrismaticVisionBackbone(nn.Module):\\\\\\\\n    def __init__(\\\\\\\\n        self,\\\\\\\\n        use_fused_vision_backbone: bool,\\\\\\\\n        image_sizes: List[int],\\\\\\\\n        timm_model_ids: List[str],\\\\\\\\n        timm_override_act_layers: List[Optional[str]],\\\\\\\\n    ) -> None:\\\\\\\\n        super().__init__()\\\\\\\\n        self.use_fused_vision_backbone = use_fused_vision_backbone\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        # [Contract] Validate number of (fused) vision backbones, create \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"alpha\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" featurizer and Instantiate\\\\\\\\n        #   =>> Note :: Monkey-Patch the `forward()` function of the backbone to ensure FSDP-compatibility\\\\\\\\n        #               Hardcodes `get_intermediate_layers` to return the **SECOND-TO-LAST** layer patches!\\\\\\\\n        assert len(timm_model_ids) <= 2, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Prismatic models only support up to 2 (fused) vision backbones!\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n        self.featurizer = timm.create_model(\\\\\\\\n            timm_model_ids[0],\\\\\\\\n            pretrained=False,\\\\\\\\n            num_classes=0,\\\\\\\\n            img_size=image_sizes[0],\\\\\\\\n            act_layer=timm_override_act_layers[0],\\\\\\\\n        )\\\\\\\\n        self.featurizer.forward = unpack_tuple(\\\\\\\\n            partial(self.featurizer.get_intermediate_layers, n={len(self.featurizer.blocks) - 2})\\\\\\\\n        )\\\\\\\\n        self.embed_dim = self.featurizer.embed_dim\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        # If `use_fused_vision_backbone` =>> create \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"beta\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" featurizer\\\\\\\\n        if self.use_fused_vision_backbone:\\\\\\\\n            self.fused_featurizer = timm.create_model(\\\\\\\\n                timm_model_ids[1],\\\\\\\\n                pretrained=False,\\\\\\\\n                num_classes=0,\\\\\\\\n                img_size=image_sizes[1],\\\\\\\\n                act_layer=timm_override_act_layers[1],\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 18001 字符] ...\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nel_values,\\\\\\\\n                \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"past_key_values\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\": past_key_values,\\\\\\\\n                \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"use_cache\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\": kwargs.get(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"use_cache\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"),\\\\\\\\n            }\\\\\\\\n        )\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        return model_inputs\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    # Defer to Language Model (all handle this differently, with different return types)\\\\\\\\n    def _reorder_cache(self, *args, **kwargs) -> Any:\\\\\\\\n        return self.language_model._reorder_cache(*args, **kwargs)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nclass OpenVLAForActionPrediction(PrismaticForConditionalGeneration):\\\\\\\\n    config_class: PretrainedConfig = OpenVLAConfig\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    def __init__(self, config: OpenVLAConfig) -> None:\\\\\\\\n        super().__init__(config)\\\\\\\\n        self.norm_stats = config.norm_stats\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        # Compute action bins\\\\\\\\n        self.bins = np.linspace(-1, 1, config.n_action_bins)\\\\\\\\n        self.bin_centers = (self.bins[:-1] + self.bins[1:]) / 2.0\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        # Compute vocab size for de-tokenization -- revert added \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"multiple of\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n        self.vocab_size = self.config.text_config.vocab_size - self.config.pad_to_multiple_of\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    def predict_action(\\\\\\\\n        self, input_ids: Optional[torch.LongTensor] = None, unnorm_key: Optional[str] = None, **kwargs: str\\\\\\\\n    ) -> np.ndarray:\\\\\\\\n        \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Thin wrapper around .generate() that decodes predicted actions and unnormalizes them.\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n        # If the special empty token ('') does not already appear after the colon (':') token in the prompt\\\\\\\\n        # (after \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"OUT:\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" or \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"ASSISTANT:\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"), insert it to match the inputs seen at training time\\\\\\\\n        if not torch.all(input_ids[:, -1] == 29871):\\\\\\\\n            input_ids = torch.cat(\\\\\\\\n                (input_ids, torch.unsqueeze(torch.Tensor([29871]).long(), dim=0).to(input_ids.device)), dim=1\\\\\\\\n            )\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        # Run VLA inference\\\\\\\\n        generated_ids = self.generate(input_ids, max_new_tokens=self.get_action_dim(unnorm_key), **kwargs)\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        # Extract predicted action tokens and translate into (normalized) continuous actions\\\\\\\\n        predicted_action_token_ids = generated_ids[0, -self.get_action_dim(unnorm_key) :].cpu().numpy()\\\\\\\\n        discretized_actions = self.vocab_size - predicted_action_token_ids\\\\\\\\n        discretized_actions = np.clip(discretized_actions - 1, a_min=0, a_max=self.bin_centers.shape[0] - 1)\\\\\\\\n        normalized_actions = self.bin_centers[discretized_actions]\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        # Unnormalize actions\\\\\\\\n        action_norm_stats = self.get_action_stats(unnorm_key)\\\\\\\\n        mask = action_norm_stats.get(\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"mask\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", np.ones_like(action_norm_stats[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"q01\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"], dtype=bool))\\\\\\\\n        action_high, action_low = np.array(action_norm_stats[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"q99\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"]), np.array(action_norm_stats[\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"q01\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"])\\\\\\\\n        actions = np.where(\\\\\\\\n            mask,\\\\\\\\n            0.5 * (normalized_actions + 1) * (action_high - action_low) + action_low,\\\\\\\\n            normalized_actions,\\\\\\\\n        )\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        return actions\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    @staticmethod\\\\\\\\n    def _check_unnorm_key(norm_stats: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]], unnorm_key: Optional[str]) -> str:\\\\\\\\n        if unnorm_key is None:\\\\\\\\n            assert len(norm_stats) == 1, (\\\\\\\\n                f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Your model was trained on more than one dataset, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n                f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"please pass a `unnorm_key` from the following options to choose the statistics \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n                f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"used for un-normalizing actions: {norm_stats.keys()}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n            )\\\\\\\\n            unnorm_key = next(iter(norm_stats.keys()))\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n        assert unnorm_key in norm_stats, (\\\\\\\\n            f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"The `unnorm_key` you chose is not in the set of available dataset statistics, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n            f\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"please choose from: {norm_stats.keys()}\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n        )\\\\\\\\n        return unnorm_key\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    def get_action_dim(self, unnorm_key: Optional[str] = None) -> int:\\\\\\\\n        \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Get the dimensionality of the policy's action space.\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n        unnorm_key = self._check_unnorm_key(self.norm_stats, unnorm_key)\\\\\\\\n        return len(self.norm_stats[unnorm_key][\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"action\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"][\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"q01\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"])\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\n    def get_action_stats(self, unnorm_key: Optional[str] = None) ->
